// The sidebar layout editor. Changes save as they are made — rail order is a
// low-stakes preference, so there is no draft/Save/Discard state to lose.

export default function RailLayoutEditor() {
  const { prefs, save, saving, loading, error, retry, canSave, ownerId } = useRailPrefs();
  const permissions = useUserPermissions();
  const [justSaved, setJustSaved] = React.useState(false);
  const savedTimer = React.useRef<number | null>(null);
  const ownerRef = React.useRef(ownerId);
  ownerRef.current = ownerId;

  React.useEffect(() => {
    setJustSaved(false);
    if (savedTimer.current) window.clearTimeout(savedTimer.current);
  }, [ownerId]);

  React.useEffect(() => {
    return () => {
      if (savedTimer.current) window.clearTimeout(savedTimer.current);
    };
  }, []);

  const commit = async (next: RailPrefs) => {
    const savingOwner = ownerId;
    setJustSaved(false);
    try {
      await save(next);
      if (ownerRef.current !== savingOwner) return;
      setJustSaved(true);
      if (savedTimer.current) window.clearTimeout(savedTimer.current);
      savedTimer.current = window.setTimeout(() => setJustSaved(false), 1600);
    } catch {
      if (ownerRef.current !== savingOwner) return;
      toast.error("Could not save your layout. Please try again.");
    }
  };

  // Same predicate as the live rail, so the preview never padlocks a

# Reading Center build: lessons from review

Gemini reads this file **before starting every chunk**. Claude appends a lesson after each review that
found a mistake. Each lesson is a rule to follow from now on. Newest at the bottom. Never delete lines.

1. Never stash, restore or switch branches in the shared checkout `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os-projects\lsatu`. Work only in `lsatu-reading-center`.
2. When a plan-supplied test fails, stop and hand off with the exact failure (as you did in chunk B). Do not edit the test or the code to force a pass. The plan can be wrong; the supervisor decides.
3. Before handing off, run `git status --short`; it must be empty. Commit any work in progress with a `WIP:` prefix rather than leaving it uncommitted.
4. Reading font, size and line spacing apply ONLY to passage text. `ReaderSurface` exposes them as CSS variables; put `style={READING_TEXT_STYLE}` (exported from `components/ReaderSurface.tsx`) on the element that holds the passage words. Never on bars, buttons or dialogs.
---

## Rules you must follow the whole time

1. **Branch, never `main`.** Step 0 creates the branch. Never run `git push origin main`, never
   `git reset --hard`, never `git push --force`.
2. **Touch only the files this plan names.** The one exception: a test file next to a file you created.
   Do not reformat, rename or "clean up" any other file.
3. **Do not edit** `src/integrations/supabase/types.ts`, `src/integrations/supabase/client.ts`,
   `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md`, or any existing migration file.
4. **Do not apply database migrations.** Write the `.sql` file only. Do not run `supabase db push`,

5. **No new npm packages.** Everything needed is already installed (React, react-router-dom,
   @tanstack/react-query, recharts, shadcn/ui in `src/components/ui/`, lucide-react, vitest).
6. **No em dashes (—) in any text a user sees.** Use a comma, a colon or a period instead.
   (The seed passage in step 2 is the one exception: it is source text, copy it exactly.)
7. **Because the database types are not regenerated yet**, every Supabase call to a `reading_center_*`
   table must go through the helper `rcDb()` from step 4. Never write `supabase.from("reading_center_...")`
   anywhere else.
8. **Imports** use the `@/` alias, e.g. `import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";`.
9. TypeScript strict mode is off in this repo. Still give every exported function explicit parameter and
   return types.
10. Commit after every step with the exact message given. Run `npm run typecheck` before every commit;
    if it fails, fix only errors in files you created.

## Step 10: The five mode renderers (`components/modes/`)

Every mode component receives the same props:

```ts
export interface ModeProps {
  tokens: Token[];
  chunks: Chunk[];
  chunk: number;              // current chunk index
  prefs: Preferences;
  playing: boolean;
  onWordClick: (wordIndex: number) => void; // used for jump and bookmarks
}
```

Put `ModeProps` in `components/modes/types.ts`.

**Font rule (applies to every mode, the View panel body and the Sample Text preview):** the passage text
element must use `style={READING_TEXT_STYLE}` (import from `../ReaderSurface`). Never set the reading font
size anywhere else, and never apply it to the top bar, bottom bar, buttons or dialogs. For the Sample Text
preview in the preferences dialog, set the inline style from the chosen font stack and size directly.

Then create:

### 10a. `SerialMode.tsx`
- Show only `chunks[chunk].rows`. Each row is one line: the words joined by a space.
- Container: flex column, vertically centred in the stage (`flex-1 flex items-center`).
- If `prefs.center_text` then `text-center justify-center items-center`, else left-aligned with
  `pl-[15%]`.
- When `playing` is false, each word is a `<button>` (no visible button styling, `cursor-pointer`)
  that calls `onWordClick(token.index)`.

### 10b. `FlowMode.tsx`
- Build "lines": group all tokens into rows of `prefs.words_per_line` tokens, never crossing a
  paragraph (reuse `chunkTokens(tokens, prefs.words_per_line, 1)`; each chunk is one line).
- Show only the current line's words at the position they would sit: render the **whole current line**
  but give every word outside the current chunk `opacity-0` (invisible, still takes space). The current
  chunk's words are fully visible. Left-aligned with `pl-[15%]`.
- Clickable words when paused, same as Serial.

### 10c. `HighlightMode.tsx`
- Render the whole passage. Group tokens into rows of `prefs.words_per_line` words (same grouping as
  Flow). Each row is a block line.
- Words with `index` between `chunks[chunk].startWord` and `chunks[chunk].endWord` (inclusive) have
  full opacity and `font-semibold`; every other word has `opacity-35` (use `style={{ opacity: 0.35 }}`).
- Paragraphs are separated by a gap (`mb-6` on the last row of each paragraph).
- Keep the current chunk vertically centred: give the first word of the current chunk a ref and, when
  `chunk` changes, call `ref.scrollIntoView({ block: "center", behavior: prefs.wpm > 600 ? "auto" : "smooth" })`.
- All words clickable at all times when paused.

### 10d. `ScrollMode.tsx`
- Same row layout as Highlight, inside a container with `overflow-hidden` and a fixed height (`h-[70vh]`).
- Draw a thin horizontal guide line at 40% of the container height (absolute positioned,
  `border-t border-current opacity-30`).
- Translate the inner column with CSS `transform: translateY(...)` so the row containing the current
  chunk's first word sits on the guide line. Use `transition: transform 150ms linear`. Measure the row's
  `offsetTop` with a ref on the row element.
- The current chunk's words are full opacity; others `0.6`.
- Clickable words when paused.

### 10e. `EReaderMode.tsx`
- No timer. Props additionally: `page: number`, `onPageCountChange(n)`, `onPageChange(n)`,
  `searchQuery: string`.
- Render paragraphs as normal `<p>` elements inside a column `max-w-[46rem] mx-auto px-6`.
- Pagination: put the text in a container with fixed height `calc(100vh - 140px)` and CSS columns:
  `columnWidth` = container width, `columnGap: 48px`, `height` fixed. Measure `scrollWidth / (width + 48)`
  rounded up = page count; call `onPageCountChange`. Show page `p` by setting
  `scrollLeft = p * (width + 48)` on the container (overflow hidden). Re-measure on resize and when
  font, size or spacing change.
- If `searchQuery` is non-empty: wrap matching text (case-insensitive) in `<mark>`; when the query
  changes, jump to the page containing the first match (find the first `<mark>` element, compute its

import React, { useState, useEffect, useMemo } from "react";
import type { Preferences, ReaderTheme } from "../lib/types";
import { FONTS, THEME_CLASSES } from "../lib/constants";

/** Apply to the element that holds the passage text (mode renderers, View panel body, e-Reader). */
export const READING_TEXT_STYLE: React.CSSProperties = {
  fontFamily: "var(--rc-font-family)",
  fontSize: "var(--rc-font-size)",
  lineHeight: "var(--rc-line-height)",
};

export interface ReaderSurfaceProps {
  prefs: Preferences;
  children: React.ReactNode;
  className?: string;
}

export function ReaderSurface({ prefs, children, className = "" }: ReaderSurfaceProps): React.ReactElement {
  const [systemIsDark, setSystemIsDark] = useState<boolean>(() => {
    if (typeof window !== "undefined" && window.matchMedia) {
      return window.matchMedia("(prefers-color-scheme: dark)").matches;
    }
    return false;
  });

  useEffect(() => {
    if (typeof window === "undefined" || !window.matchMedia) return;
    const media = window.matchMedia("(prefers-color-scheme: dark)");
    const handler = (e: MediaQueryListEvent) => {
      setSystemIsDark(e.matches);
    };
    media.addEventListener("change", handler);
    return () => {
      media.removeEventListener("change", handler);
    };
  }, []);

  const resolvedTheme: Exclude<ReaderTheme, "system"> = useMemo(() => {
    if (prefs.theme === "system") {
      return systemIsDark ? "dark" : "light";
    }
    return prefs.theme;
  }, [prefs.theme, systemIsDark]);

  const fontStack = useMemo(() => {
    const match = FONTS.find((f) => f.label.toLowerCase() === prefs.font_family.toLowerCase());
    return match ? match.stack : "Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif";
  }, [prefs.font_family]);

  const themeClass = THEME_CLASSES[resolvedTheme] ?? THEME_CLASSES.light;

  return (
    <div
      data-theme={resolvedTheme}
      className={`min-h-screen w-full ${themeClass} ${className}`}
      // Reading styles are exposed as CSS variables, NOT applied to the whole page, so the top bar,
      // buttons and dialogs keep normal app sizing. Only the reading text uses them, via READING_TEXT_STYLE.
      style={{
        ["--rc-font-family" as string]: fontStack,
        ["--rc-font-size" as string]: `${prefs.font_size}px`,
        ["--rc-line-height" as string]: String(prefs.line_spacing),
      } as React.CSSProperties}
    >
      {children}
    </div>
  );
}

import React from "react";
import type { ModeProps } from "./types";

export function SerialMode({
  chunks,
  chunk,
  prefs,
  playing,
  onWordClick,
}: ModeProps): React.ReactElement {
  const currentChunk = chunks[chunk];
  if (!currentChunk) {
    return <div className="flex-1" />;
  }

  const containerClasses = prefs.center_text
    ? "flex-1 flex flex-col justify-center items-center text-center w-full px-6"
    : "flex-1 flex flex-col justify-center items-start text-left w-full pl-[15%] pr-6";

  return (
    <div className={containerClasses}>
      {currentChunk.rows.map((row, rowIdx) => (
        <div key={rowIdx} className="leading-tight py-1">
          {row.map((token, tokenIdx) => (
            <React.Fragment key={token.index}>
              {tokenIdx > 0 && " "}
              {!playing ? (
                <button
                  type="button"
                  onClick={() => onWordClick(token.index)}
                  className="cursor-pointer hover:underline focus:outline-none bg-transparent border-0 p-0 font-inherit text-inherit"
                >
                  {token.text}
                </button>
              ) : (
                <span>{token.text}</span>
              )}
            </React.Fragment>
          ))}
        </div>
      ))}
    </div>
  );
}
import React, { useMemo } from "react";
import type { ModeProps } from "./types";
import { chunkTokens } from "../../lib/tokenize";

export function FlowMode({
  tokens,
  chunks,
  chunk,
  prefs,
  playing,
  onWordClick,
}: ModeProps): React.ReactElement {
  const currentChunk = chunks[chunk];

  // Group tokens into lines of words_per_line tokens, never crossing paragraph
  const lines = useMemo(() => {
    return chunkTokens(tokens, prefs.words_per_line, 1);
  }, [tokens, prefs.words_per_line]);

  if (!currentChunk) {
    return <div className="flex-1" />;
  }

  // Find the line containing the current chunk's start word
  const currentLine = lines.find(
    (l) => currentChunk.startWord >= l.startWord && currentChunk.startWord <= l.endWord,
  ) ?? lines[0];

  const lineTokens = currentLine?.rows[0] ?? [];

  return (
    <div className="flex-1 flex flex-col justify-center items-start text-left w-full pl-[15%] pr-6">
      <div className="leading-tight py-1">
        {lineTokens.map((token, tokenIdx) => {
          const isCurrentWord =
            token.index >= currentChunk.startWord && token.index <= currentChunk.endWord;

          return (
            <React.Fragment key={token.index}>
              {tokenIdx > 0 && " "}
              <span className={isCurrentWord ? "opacity-100" : "opacity-0 select-none pointer-events-none"}>
                {!playing && isCurrentWord ? (
                  <button
                    type="button"
                    onClick={() => onWordClick(token.index)}
                    className="cursor-pointer hover:underline focus:outline-none bg-transparent border-0 p-0 font-inherit text-inherit"
                  >
                    {token.text}
                  </button>
                ) : (
                  <span>{token.text}</span>
                )}
              </span>
            </React.Fragment>
          );
        })}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

export type ReadingMode = "serial" | "flow" | "highlight" | "scroll" | "ereader";
export type ReaderTheme = "light" | "dark" | "high_contrast" | "system";

export interface Passage {
  id: string;
  slug: string;
  title: string;
  author: string | null;
  body: string;
  cover_url: string | null;
  word_count: number;
  sort_order: number;
  is_published: boolean;
  created_at: string;
  updated_at: string;
}

export interface Preferences {
  wpm: number;
  reading_mode: ReadingMode;
  number_of_lines: number;
  words_per_line: number;
  center_text: boolean;
  theme: ReaderTheme;
  font_family: string;
  font_size: number;
  line_spacing: number;
  punctuation_pauses: boolean;
  speed_variability: boolean;
  goal_wpm: number;
}

export interface Progress {
  passage_id: string;
  word_index: number;
  completed_at: string | null;
  last_opened_at: string;
  hidden_from_recent: boolean;
}

export interface Bookmark {
  id: string;
  passage_id: string;
  word_index: number;
  created_at: string;
}

export interface ReadingSession {
  id: string;
  passage_id: string;
  reading_mode: ReadingMode;
  wpm: number;
  words_read: number;
  active_ms: number;
  paused_ms: number;
  started_at: string;
  ended_at: string;
}

export interface Token {
  index: number;          // position in the whole passage, starting at 0
  text: string;           // the word with its punctuation attached
  paragraphIndex: number; // starting at 0
  endsSentence: boolean;
  hasPunctuation: boolean;
  endsParagraph: boolean;
}

export interface Chunk {
  index: number;          // chunk number, starting at 0
  startWord: number;      // token index of the first word
  endWord: number;        // token index of the last word (inclusive)
  rows: Token[][];        // numberOfLines rows, each up to wordsPerLine tokens
}

import { useState, useRef, useEffect, useCallback } from "react";

export function useReadingClock(args: {
  durations: number[];        // from chunkDurations
  chunkCount: number;
  initialChunk: number;
  onChunkChange: (chunk: number) => void;
  onFinish: () => void;
}): {
  playing: boolean;
  chunk: number;
  play: () => void;
  pause: () => void;
  toggle: () => void;
  goTo: (chunk: number) => void;   // clamps to 0..chunkCount-1, keeps playing state
  next: () => void;
  prev: () => void;
  restart: () => void;             // goTo(0) and pause
  activeMs: () => number;          // total ms spent playing since mount
  pausedMs: () => number;          // total ms spent paused since the first play
} {
  const [chunk, setChunk] = useState<number>(args.initialChunk);
  const [playing, setPlaying] = useState<boolean>(false);

  const chunkRef = useRef<number>(args.initialChunk);
  const playingRef = useRef<boolean>(false);
  const durationsRef = useRef<number[]>(args.durations);
  const chunkCountRef = useRef<number>(args.chunkCount);
  const onChunkChangeRef = useRef<(chunk: number) => void>(args.onChunkChange);
  const onFinishRef = useRef<() => void>(args.onFinish);

  const animFrameIdRef = useRef<number | null>(null);
  const elapsedInChunkRef = useRef<number>(0);
  const activeMsRef = useRef<number>(0);
  const pausedMsRef = useRef<number>(0);
  const hasPlayedOnceRef = useRef<boolean>(false);
  const pauseStartRef = useRef<number | null>(null);
  const lastFrameRef = useRef<number>(0);

  useEffect(() => {
    durationsRef.current = args.durations;
    chunkCountRef.current = args.chunkCount;
    onChunkChangeRef.current = args.onChunkChange;
    onFinishRef.current = args.onFinish;
  });

  const tick = useCallback((now: number) => {
    if (!playingRef.current) return;

    let delta = now - lastFrameRef.current;
    lastFrameRef.current = now;
    if (delta > 1000) delta = 1000;
    if (delta < 0) delta = 0;

    elapsedInChunkRef.current += delta;
    activeMsRef.current += delta;

    let currentChunk = chunkRef.current;
    const durations = durationsRef.current;
    const count = chunkCountRef.current;

    let advanced = false;
    while (
      currentChunk < count &&
      elapsedInChunkRef.current >= (durations[currentChunk] ?? 0)
    ) {
      elapsedInChunkRef.current -= (durations[currentChunk] ?? 0);
      currentChunk++;
      advanced = true;
    }

    if (currentChunk >= count) {
      const lastChunk = Math.max(0, count - 1);
      chunkRef.current = lastChunk;
      setChunk(lastChunk);
      playingRef.current = false;
      setPlaying(false);
      animFrameIdRef.current = null;
      pauseStartRef.current = performance.now();
      if (advanced) {
        onChunkChangeRef.current(lastChunk);
      }
      onFinishRef.current();
      return;
    }

    if (advanced) {
      chunkRef.current = currentChunk;
      setChunk(currentChunk);
      onChunkChangeRef.current(currentChunk);
    }

    animFrameIdRef.current = requestAnimationFrame(tick);

      pauseStartRef.current = null;
    }
    hasPlayedOnceRef.current = true;
    playingRef.current = true;
    setPlaying(true);
    lastFrameRef.current = performance.now();
    animFrameIdRef.current = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }, [tick]);

    if (animFrameIdRef.current !== null) {
      cancelAnimationFrame(animFrameIdRef.current);
      animFrameIdRef.current = null;

/**
 * Headless assign: put an Activity, a guided drill, or a plain message into a
 * student's LSAT U account from the command line.
 *
 * Run with vite-node so `@/` resolves and this calls the app's OWN assignWork()
 * rather than a second implementation of it. That matters: assigning touches
 * seven tables (assignment snapshot, classroom item, conversation, message,
 * notification, plus the email edge function) and rolls back on failure. A
 * reimplementation would drift from the UI the first time the app changed.
 *
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --activity "Read, Recall, Colour"
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --drill "main-conclusion-1"
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --message "Start with PT105.S2."
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --section PT156.S2 --due 2026-09-13
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --set "PT156.S2 (LR)"
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --activity "..." --dry-run
 *
 * Credentials are read from ~/ai-os/.env (git-ignored), never from this repo's
 * .env, which IS tracked in git. Same convention as scripts/db-pull.mjs.
 */
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
import { assignWork } from "@/lib/assignWork";
import { BOOTCAMPS } from "@/lib/bootcamps";
import { updateClassroomItem } from "@/lib/classroomItems";

const REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");

function readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> {
  const out: Record<string, string> = {};
  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;
  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").split(/\r?\n/)) {
    const t = line.trim();
    if (!t || t.startsWith("#")) continue;
    const i = t.indexOf("=");
    if (i < 0) continue;
    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, "");
  }

const env = {
  ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, "../../.env")),
  ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, ".env")),
};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- arguments

function arg(name: string): string | undefined {
  const i = process.argv.indexOf(`--${name}`);
  return i >= 0 ? process.argv[i + 1] : undefined;
}
const has = (name: string) => process.argv.includes(`--${name}`);

const studentQuery = arg("student");
const activityQuery = arg("activity");
const drillQuery = arg("drill");
const messageText = arg("message");
const sectionQuery = arg("section");
const setQuery = arg("set");
const bootcampQuery = arg("bootcamp");
const scheduleFor = arg("schedule");
const dueOn = arg("due");
const dryRun = has("dry-run");

const kinds = [activityQuery, drillQuery, messageText, sectionQuery, setQuery, bootcampQuery].filter(Boolean).length;
if (!studentQuery || kinds !== 1) {
  console.error(
    "usage: vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student <name|email> \\\n" +
      "         (--activity <title> | --drill <slug|title> | --message <text>\\\n" +
      "          | --section PT<n>.S<n> | --set <title|id> | --bootcamp <id|title>)\\\n" +
      "         [--schedule YYYY-MM-DD] [--due YYYY-MM-DD] [--dry-run]",
  );
  process.exit(2);
}
for (const [flag, v] of [["schedule", scheduleFor], ["due", dueOn]] as const) {
  if (v && !/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/.test(v)) {
    console.error(`--${flag} must be YYYY-MM-DD, got "${v}"`);
    process.exit(2);
  }
}
if (dueOn && scheduleFor && dueOn < scheduleFor) {
  console.error(`--due ${dueOn} is before --schedule ${scheduleFor}; she could never do it in time.`);
  process.exit(2);
}

// --------------------------------------------------------------- signing in

/**
 * Sign in as Joshua. The service_role key would bypass RLS entirely, so it is
 * only honoured when explicitly present; the default is a normal admin login,
 * which exercises the same permission path the UI does. A script that quietly
 * ran as service_role would hide an RLS regression instead of hitting it.
 */
async function signIn(): Promise<string> {
  if (env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY) {
    console.warn(
      "! SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY is set but this script signs in as the admin user.\n" +
        "  Using it would require injecting a second client into assignWork, which would\n" +
        "  bypass RLS for every write. Falling back to the admin login.",
    );
  }

[external_agent_tool_result]
 * Group tokens into chunks of up to wordsPerLine * numberOfLines words.
 * A chunk never crosses a paragraph boundary.
 */
export function chunkTokens(tokens: Token[], wordsPerLine: number, numberOfLines: number): Chunk[] {
  const size = Math.max(1, wordsPerLine) * Math.max(1, numberOfLines);
  const chunks: Chunk[] = [];
  let current: Token[] = [];
  const flush = () => {
    if (current.length === 0) return;
    const rows: Token[][] = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < current.length; i += wordsPerLine) rows.push(current.slice(i, i + wordsPerLine));
    chunks.push({
      index: chunks.length,
      startWord: current[0].index,
      endWord: current[current.length - 1].index,
      rows,
    });
    current = [];
  };
  for (const t of tokens) {
    current.push(t);
    if (current.length === size || t.endsParagraph) flush();
  }
  flush();
  return chunks;
}

/** Index of the chunk that contains wordIndex. Returns 0 if not found. */
export function chunkIndexForWord(chunks: Chunk[], wordIndex: number): number {
  const i = chunks.findIndex((c) => wordIndex >= c.startWord && wordIndex <= c.endWord);
  return i === -1 ? 0 : i;
[/external_agent_tool_result]

116:  $rc$The rationalizing of society can be conceptualized as the pursuit of efficiency, predictability, calculability, and control through technology. But rational systems inevitably spawn a series of irrationalities that result in the compromising and perhaps even the undermining of their rationality. Fast-food restaurants, which epitomize the rational model, proffer the fastest means of getting from a hungry state to a sated one, without surprises, at low cost, in a carnival-like setting suggesting that fun awaits the consumer at each visit. The wholesomeness of the food seems an insignificant consideration. Whereas in the past, working people were prepared to spend up to an hour preparing dinner, they now are impatient if a meal is not on the table within ten minutes. (For their part, some fast-food restaurants have developed chairs that become uncomfortable after about twenty minutes, to ensure that diners do not stay long.) Fast-food restaurants have preferentially recruited adolescent help, at least until recently, because this age group adjusts more easily than adults do to surrendering their autonomy to machines, rules, and procedures. Few skills are required on the job, so workers are asked to use only a minute portion of their abilities. This policy is irrational from the standpoint of the organization, since it could obtain much more from its employees for the money (however negligible) it pays them. These minimal skill demands are also irrational from the perspective of the employees, who are not allowed to think or to respond creatively to the demands of the work. These restrictions lead to high levels of resentment, job dissatisfaction, alienation, absenteeism, and turnover among workers in fast-food franchises. In fact, these businesses have the highest turnover rate of any industry in the U.S. The entire workforce of the fast-food industry turns over three times in a year. Although the simple, repetitive nature of the work makes it easy to replace those who leave, the organization would clearly benefit from keepi...

anchor='            <Route path="/admin/drive-files" element={\n'
assert s.count(anchor)==1

                <React.Suspense fallback={<div className="min-h-screen" />}>
                  <ReadingCenterReader />

s=s.replace(anchor,'''                      {/* Tools: standalone utilities. Reading Center ships admin-only. */}
                      <Route path="tools" element={
                        <React.Suspense fallback={<div />}><TeacherToolsRoom /></React.Suspense>
                      } />
                      <Route path="tools/reading-center" element={
                        <React.Suspense fallback={<div />}><ReadingCenterLibrary /></React.Suspense>

                      <Route path="tools/reading-center/stats" element={

                      <Route path="tools/reading-center/manage" element={

'''+anchor,1)
open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s)

## assistant

Steps 10 through 17 are built and committed on `feat/reading-center`, and the Tools room is in the Teacher Workspace. Typecheck, a full `npm run build` and the 14 tests all pass. I haven't seen any of it working in the browser yet: the dev server stops at the sign-in page, and I won't type your real admin password, even on localhost.

**What's built**
- **Tools room:** a new "Tools" tab at the end of the workspace tabs, after Insights, at `/teacher/tools`. Reading Center is its first card. Adding a future tool means adding one row to `src/pages/teacher/tools/TeacherToolsRoom.tsx`.
- **Pages:** library, stats and passage manager open inside the workspace. The reader opens full screen, like the Whiteboard. It sits at `.../read/<passage>`, so a passage titled "Stats" can't collide with the stats page. Everything is still admin-only.
- **Step 10 fixes:** all five reading modes now use your font, size and line spacing. Before, they ignored them, and some styles overrode line spacing. e-Reader page-turning was broken (invalid CSS) and its search skipped matches; both are fixed.
- **Steps 11–17:**
  - the reader controls: speed dialog, settings, full-text view, bookmarks, shortcuts, finish card, "pick up where you left off"
  - the reader page
  - the library
  - stats with goal setting
  - the passage manager
  - the bulk import script
- **Import script:** the dry run found my test file and printed one NEW line without writing anything. That was required, because the database tables don't exist yet.

**Where I departed from the plan**
- **Flow mode:** the plan's version looked exactly like Serial. Following the feature inventory, each chunk now appears at its real position on a line of about 10 words.
- **Reordering passages:** up and down arrows instead of drag-and-drop. Nothing in the app uses the drag library, so there was no pattern to copy.
- **Saving progress:** nothing is saved until you choose Resume or Start over, so opening a passage can't wipe saved progress.
- **Import credentials:** `assign.ts` looks for your `.env` in `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\.env`, which doesn't exist. Your keys are in `dev\ai-os\.env`. The import script now reads from there. `assign.ts` itself probably fails the same way, but I didn't touch it.

**Needs your attention**
- **Build side effect:** `npm run build` cut `supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts` down by 589 lines in the worktree. The Lovable MCP plugin rewrites that file during every build. My restore was denied, so it's sitting uncommitted. Run this to restore it:
```bash
git -C C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-reading-center checkout -- supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts
```
- The shared `lsatu` checkout shows the same modified file, which probably has the same cause. I didn't touch it.
- **Migration:** still not applied. Until it is, the library will show "Could not load passages" and the reader can't open anything. Applying it changes the live Lovable Cloud database, so I need your yes right before I do it.

**Not done**
- Step 18 (final verification against the spec's acceptance checks) and Step 19 (push and open the PR). The branch is unpushed.
- A real browser test. I started the dev server at `http://localhost:8098` and added `lsatu-reading-center-dev` to `.claude/launch.json`. If you sign in there, I'll go through every mode, dialog and keyboard shortcut. That's only useful once the migration is applied.

## assistant

You're signed in now, so I got further. Everything that doesn't need the database works. Everything that does is blocked on the migration.

**What works**
- **Tools tab:** it's on the workspace rail, and the Tools room shows the Reading Center card. Clicking the card opens the library.
- **Library:** the header, Stats button, Manage passages button and search box all render. Then it shows "Could not load passages: Could not find the table 'public.reading_center_passages'". That's expected, because the migration hasn't been applied.
- **Stats:** the page renders with the period picker, the Set goal button and both speed gauges (current 0, target 600). That's the default goal, since your preferences can't be saved yet.
- **Manage passages:** the page renders. Its passage list will hit the same missing-table error.
- **Errors:** none in the browser console or the dev server log.

**One layout problem.** At the in-app browser's width, the rail now has 8 tabs. "Tools" is cut off at the right edge, and you have to scroll the rail to see it. On a wider screen it should fit. If you'd rather it never hides, I could shorten "Teaching Assistant" to "Assistant" (the header already has an Assistant button), or put Tools earlier in the order.

**The next step is the migration.** It creates the five `reading_center_*` tables plus the seed passage in the live Lovable Cloud database. Once it's in, I can test the reader itself: all five modes, the dialogs, bookmarks, resume and the keyboard shortcuts.

The Lovable Cloud rule says database changes go through Lovable. As far as I know, the free path is pasting the SQL into Lovable's SQL editor yourself, which costs no credits. Should I apply it, and by which route?

import { useQuery, useMutation, useQueryClient, type UseQueryResult, type UseMutationResult } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { useAuth } from "@/contexts/AuthContext";
import { rcDb } from "../lib/db";
import { DEFAULT_PREFERENCES } from "../lib/constants";
import { countWords } from "../lib/tokenize";
import type {
  Passage,
  Preferences,
  Progress,
  Bookmark,
  ReadingSession,
} from "../lib/types";

export function usePassages(): UseQueryResult<Passage[], Error> {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const userId = user?.id;

  return useQuery({
    queryKey: ["reading-center", "passages"],
    queryFn: async (): Promise<Passage[]> => {
      const { data, error } = await rcDb("reading_center_passages")
        .select("*")
        .order("sort_order")
        .order("title");
      if (error) throw error;
      return (data ?? []) as Passage[];
    },
    enabled: !!userId,
  });
}

export function usePassageBySlug(slug: string | undefined): UseQueryResult<Passage | null, Error> {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const userId = user?.id;

  return useQuery({
    queryKey: ["reading-center", "passage", slug],
    queryFn: async (): Promise<Passage | null> => {
      if (!slug) return null;
      const { data, error } = await rcDb("reading_center_passages")
        .select("*")
        .eq("slug", slug)
        .maybeSingle();
      if (error) throw error;
      return (data ?? null) as Passage | null;
    },
    enabled: !!userId && !!slug,
  });
}

export function usePreferences(): UseQueryResult<Preferences, Error> {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const userId = user?.id;

  return useQuery({

export function useSavePreferences(): UseMutationResult<Preferences, Error, Partial<Preferences>> {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const userId = user?.id;
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();

  return useMutation({
    mutationFn: async (input: Partial<Preferences>): Promise<Preferences> => {
      if (!userId) throw new Error("Not authenticated");
      const current =
        queryClient.getQueryData<Preferences>(["reading-center", "prefs", userId]) ?? DEFAULT_PREFERENCES;
      const merged: Preferences = { ...current, ...input };
      const payload = {
        user_id: userId,
        ...merged,
        updated_at: new Date().toISOString(),
      };
      const { error } = await rcDb("reading_center_preferences").upsert(payload, {
        onConflict: "user_id",
      });
      if (error) throw error;
      return merged;
    },
    onMutate: async (input: Partial<Preferences>) => {
      if (!userId) return;
      await queryClient.cancelQueries({ queryKey: ["reading-center", "prefs", userId] });
      const previous =
        queryClient.getQueryData<Preferences>(["reading-center", "prefs", userId]) ?? DEFAULT_PREFERENCES;
      const merged: Preferences = { ...previous, ...input };
      queryClient.setQueryData(["reading-center", "prefs", userId], merged);
      return { previous };
    },
    onError: (_err, _input, context) => {
      if (context?.previous && userId) {
        queryClient.setQueryData(["reading-center", "prefs", userId], context.previous);
      }
    },
    onSettled: () => {
      if (userId) {
        queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ["reading-center", "prefs", userId] });
      }
    },
  });
}

export function useProgressList(): UseQueryResult<Progress[], Error> {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const userId = user?.id;

  return useQuery({
    queryKey: ["reading-center", "progress", userId],
    queryFn: async (): Promise<Progress[]> => {
      if (!userId) return [];
      const { data, error } = await rcDb("reading_center_progress")
        .select("*")
        .eq("user_id", userId);
      if (error) throw error;
export function useBookmarks(passageId: string | undefined): UseQueryResult<Bookmark[], Error> {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const userId = user?.id;

  return useQuery({
    queryKey: ["reading-center", "bookmarks", userId, passageId],
    queryFn: async (): Promise<Bookmark[]> => {
      if (!userId || !passageId) return [];
      const { data, error } = await rcDb("reading_center_bookmarks")
        .select("*")
        .eq("user_id", userId)
        .eq("passage_id", passageId)
        .order("created_at", { ascending: false });
      if (error) throw error;
      return (data ?? []) as Bookmark[];
    },
    enabled: !!userId && !!passageId,
  });
}

export function useAddBookmark(): UseMutationResult<Bookmark, Error, { passage_id: string; word_index: number }> {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const userId = user?.id;
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();

  return useMutation({
    mutationFn: async (input: { passage_id: string; word_index: number }): Promise<Bookmark> => {
      if (!userId) throw new Error("Not authenticated");
      const { data, error } = await rcDb("reading_center_bookmarks")
        .insert({
          user_id: userId,
          passage_id: input.passage_id,
          word_index: input.word_index,
        })
        .select()
        .single();
      if (error) throw error;
      return data as Bookmark;
    },
    onSuccess: (_data, vars) => {
      if (userId) {
        queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ["reading-center", "bookmarks", userId, vars.passage_id] });
      }
    },
  });
}

import { BrowserRouter, NavLink, Outlet, Route, Routes } from "react-router-dom";
import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { Toaster } from "sonner";
import { TooltipProvider } from "@/components/ui/tooltip";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";
import LibraryPage from "@/features/reading-center/pages/LibraryPage";
import ReaderPage from "@/features/reading-center/pages/ReaderPage";
import StatsPage from "@/features/reading-center/pages/StatsPage";
import AdminPassagesPage from "@/features/reading-center/pages/AdminPassagesPage";
import { RC_LIBRARY_PATH, RC_MANAGE_PATH, RC_STATS_PATH } from "@/features/reading-center/lib/routes";

const queryClient = new QueryClient({ defaultOptions: { queries: { retry: false, refetchOnWindowFocus: false } } });

const NAV = [
  { to: RC_LIBRARY_PATH, label: "Library", end: true },
  { to: RC_STATS_PATH, label: "Stats" },
  { to: RC_MANAGE_PATH, label: "Manage passages" },
];

/** Library, stats and passage manager share this frame; the reader is full screen. */
function AppFrame() {
  return (
    <div className="min-h-screen">
      <header className="border-b border-black/10">
        <div className="mx-auto flex max-w-6xl items-center gap-6 px-5 sm:px-6">
          <span className="py-3.5 font-display text-lg tracking-tight">Reading Center</span>
          <nav className="flex gap-1 overflow-x-auto" aria-label="Reading Center">
            {NAV.map(({ to, label, end }) => (
              <NavLink
                key={to}
                to={to}
                end={end}
                className={({ isActive }) =>
                  cn(
                    "shrink-0 border-b-[3px] px-3 py-3.5 text-sm font-medium transition",
                    isActive ? "border-black text-black" : "border-transparent text-black/50 hover:text-black",
                  )
                }
              >
                {label}
              </NavLink>
            ))}
          </nav>
        </div>
      </header>
      <main>
        <Outlet />
      </main>
    </div>
  );
}

export default function App() {
  return (
    <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
      <TooltipProvider>
        <BrowserRouter>
          <Routes>
            <Route element={<AppFrame />}>
              <Route path={RC_LIBRARY_PATH} element={<LibraryPage />} />
              <Route path={RC_STATS_PATH} element={<StatsPage />} />
              <Route path={RC_MANAGE_PATH} element={<AdminPassagesPage />} />
            </Route>
            <Route path="/read/:slug" element={<ReaderPage />} />
            <Route path="*" element={<LibraryPage />} />
          </Routes>
        </BrowserRouter>
        <Toaster position="bottom-center" />
      </TooltipProvider>
    </QueryClientProvider>
  );
}
EOF

# Reading Center (standalone)

Speed-reading web app, built standalone first and integrated into LSAT U later
(Joshua, 2026-10-13). Spec: `dev/ai-os/brainstorms/2026-10-12-reading-center-build-spec.md`.

- `src/features/reading-center/` is the same feature folder as LSAT U branch
  `feat/reading-center` (worktree `dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-reading-center`). Keep the two in step.
- Data lives in browser storage (`lib/store.ts`). `hooks/useReadingCenterData.ts`
  keeps the LSAT U hook names and shapes; integration swaps that one file back to
  Supabase and changes `lib/routes.ts`. Nothing else should know where data lives.
- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port 8100.
- No em dashes in user-facing text.
EOF

## assistant

Reading Center now runs as its own web app at **http://localhost:8100**, completely separate from LSAT U. It works right away: passages, settings and progress save in your browser, so it doesn't need the database migration.

**Checked in the browser**
- **Library:** shows the seed passage. "Living in a rational society", 550 words.
- **Serial:** plays, and Space pauses it. Progress saved at the word I stopped on.
- **Settings:** the S key opens them. Switching to Highlight kept my place in the passage.
- **Resume:** reopening the passage asked "Pick up where you left off?" and Resume returned to the saved word.
- **e-Reader:** pages turn with the arrow keys (1/3, then 2/3), and the text continues cleanly across the break.
- **Flow:** each chunk appears at its real place on the line.
- **Scroll:** the current line sits on the guide line.
- **Build:** typecheck, 14/14 tests and a production build all pass.

**Not checked by hand:** bookmarks, the View panel, the WPM dialog, the Stats charts with real data, and the passage manager (adding, editing, reordering, deleting). They're built but untested. Stats will stay empty until you've done some reading.

**What else changed**
- **LSAT U:** I took the Tools room back out of the `feat/reading-center` branch. The feature code is still on that branch, unused, ready for when we add it to LSAT U.
- **New project:** the app is at `dev/ai-os-projects/reading-center`, in its own local git repo with no GitHub remote yet. Its reading feature is the same code as the LSAT U branch. Only the save layer and the page addresses differ, so integrating later means swapping those two files back.
- **Small fix:** the slider tracks were nearly invisible, so I darkened them.
- **Restart config:** `reading-center-dev` is in `.claude/launch.json` if the server ever needs restarting.

**When it goes into LSAT U:** "assign" will mean turning on access to the whole tool for a student, as you said. That's also when the database migration gets applied.

**Still open**
1. **Build side effect on the LSAT U branch:** the earlier LSAT U build mangled `supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts` in the `lsatu-reading-center` worktree, and that change is still uncommitted. The restore command from before still works:
   ```bash
   git -C C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-reading-center checkout -- supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts
   ```
2. **Delete-passage wording:** it still says "N students have progress". In a one-person app it should say "You have progress".
3. **Duplicate links:** the Library page repeats the Stats and Manage links that are already in the top nav. I left both in because the page is shared with the LSAT U copy.

---
title: Spreeder feature inventory
date: 2026-10-12
source: https://spreeder.ereflect.com (Joshua's logged-in account, inspected live in Chrome)
purpose: Reference spec for replicating Spreeder in a skill / LSAT U reading tool with predetermined passages
---

# Spreeder feature inventory

Verified live unless marked *(code only)*. "Code only" means the name exists in the app's main bundle
(`resources.ereflect.com/Spreeder/webApp/main.21893fdef5a7593c.js`) but no screen for it was reachable
from this account.

## 1. Library (`/library`)

- Shelves: **Recent**, **Added By Me** (with "View All").
- Card: cover image (generic document icon for user text), title, author line.
- Card ⋮ menu: **View/Location**, **Edit Image**, **Edit Text**, **Tag**, **Delete**, **Hide from Recent**.
- Global search: "Searching 20504 books.." (built-in catalogue) plus a book-search icon.
- Routes: `library`, `library/:name`, `library/tags/:name`.

## 2. Adding text (Add Text button)

| Option | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| Paste Text | Full-screen "Add To Library": Enter Title field, Add Text Here box, Cancel / Save (Save disabled until filled). |
| Import Document | Opens a file picker (accepted types not exposed). |
| AI Book Summary | Uses AI credits; generates a cover step, falls back to text only if the cover fails. |
| AI Generate | Uses AI credits. |

Profile shows the credit balance ("0 AI Credits").

## 3. Reader (`/spreed`)

### Chrome around the text
- Top: Back, title, **Search** (e-Reader only), **Fullscreen**, **Bookmarks**, **Settings (gear)**.
- Bottom: **Keyboard shortcuts** button, **WPM button** (shows current speed), **View**, progress ("1% - 3 minutes remaining").
- Transport: previous chunk ◁, play/pause, next chunk ▷.

### Speed (WPM) dialog
- "Reading Speed (WPM)" with **−100, −10, +10, +100** buttons, Cancel / Save. Default 200.

### View panel
- Full passage overlay with "User added" badge, **Search this text**, current word boxed, click any word to jump,
  play button, footer "3 minutes to go - 200 WPM".

### Bookmarks
- Empty state: "To create a bookmark, simply pause Spreeder while you're reading and long press or click on any word."

### Keyboard shortcuts
| Keys | Action |
|---|---|
| ↑ / ↓ or + / − | increase / decrease speed |
| P or Space | pause / resume |
| → | section forward |
| ← | section back |
| R | restart |
| S | settings |

## 4. Preferences dialog ("<name>'s preferences")

Tabs: **Spreed · Appearance · Advanced**, plus **Restore Defaults**. Saved per user, server-side.

### Spreed tab: Reading Mode and its per-mode settings
| Mode | What it looks like | Settings (ranges) |
|---|---|---|
| **Serial** | One chunk flashed in a fixed spot | Number of Lines (1–6), Words Per Line (1–6), Center Text (toggle, default on) |

| **Highlight** | Whole text visible, current chunk bright, rest dimmed | Words Per Line (1–6), Number of Lines (1–6) |
| **Scroll** | Text scrolls past | Words Per Line (1–6) |

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Dump the webpack modules holding each reading mode
command: cd C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/95fbd50f-6898-4412-a19a-7fd02386eb44/scratchpad/spreeder-css && python -I - main.js ../spreeder-code <<'EOF'
import sys,re
js=open(sys.argv[1],encoding='utf-8').read(); out=sys.argv[2]
def dump(name,start,end):
    seg=js[start:end]
    # light pretty: newline after ; and { }
    seg=re.sub(r';',';\n',seg)
    open(f"{out}/{name}.js","w",encoding="utf-8").write(seg)
    print(name,len(seg))
# module boundaries: webpack modules look like ,12345:(tt,b,e)=>{ ... ; find module containing offset
mods=[(m.start(),m.group(1)) for m in re.finditer(r'[,{](\d{2,6}):\((?:\w+,){1,2}\w+\)=>\{',js)]
def module_at(off):
    prev=[m for m in mods if m[0]<=off][-1]; nxt=[m for m in mods if m[0]>off][0]
    return prev[0],nxt[0],prev[1]
for name,off in [("ereader",39131),("serial",65276),("base-activity",256650),("text-highlighter",4635878)]:
    s,e,mid=module_at(off); dump(f"{name}.{mid}",s,e)
# serial extends a base class i.p — the flow subclass sits at 60515 region
s,e,mid=module_at(60515); dump(f"flow-subclass.{mid}",s,e)
EOF

(Light and Dark), preferences dialog, View panel, e-Reader search, play timing (about 310 ms a word at 200 WPM,

## assistant

Reading Center is rebuilt to copy Spreeder's look and behaviour, and it's running at http://localhost:8100. It follows Spreeder's actual code and live screens, not my earlier guesses. Typecheck passes, 28/28 tests pass, the production build succeeds, and it's committed as `441536b`.

**How I matched it**
- **Live capture:** I went through Spreeder screen by screen in your logged-in Chrome, recording exact colours, fonts and sizes in all four themes.
- **Code analysis:** I downloaded the app's full code (the main bundle plus 219 lazy-loaded chunks) and ran it through REA. REA's server didn't connect this session, so I used its command-line version.
- **Spec:** everything is written up in [research/2026-10-13-spreeder-reconstruction-spec.md](research/2026-10-13-spreeder-reconstruction-spec.md).

**What I got wrong before, now fixed**
- Colours and font: Spreeder's real palette and Poppins replace LSAT U's grey look, across Light, Dark, High Contrast (charcoal, not black and yellow) and System.
- Flow: it reveals each chunk left to right; it doesn't flash chunks across a line.
- Highlight: the current word isn't bold.
- Speed Variability: it goes by chunk size, not word length.
- Punctuation pauses: they apply at every group size.
- Resume: there's no "pick up where you left off" prompt; it just resumes.
- R: it restarts the current section, not the whole passage.
- Recommend: it's a reading test; I had made up a "3× your speed" formula.

**The reader now works like Spreeder's**
- The timing formula is Spreeder's: at 200 WPM a word shows for about 310 ms, and punctuated words stay up twice as long.
- It uses the same timer approach, so reading doesn't slow down in a background tab.
- Clicking the reading area pauses or resumes. Clicking a word opens Bookmark/Note.
- The View panel and e-Reader search work as in Spreeder. The same passage even paginates to the same "1 / 6" pages.

**Checked in the browser**
- Library
- Serial playback and timing
- Preferences dialog
- Highlight mode
- e-Reader with search
- View panel
- Light and Dark themes

Not clicked through yet: Flow, Scroll, the Profile page, the Add Text screens and the card-menu dialogs.

**Where I deliberately differ from Spreeder**
- **Flow:** Spreeder's own Flow is broken. Its wipe uses the time between frames instead of elapsed time, so it shows almost nothing. I built the reveal it was clearly meant to do, since you said every feature should work.
- **Branding:** no Spreeder logo, cover art or their private icon font. Matching open-source icons stand in.

**Not built yet**
- **Need an outside service:** Look Up Word, Add to Wordela Word List, AI Book Summary, AI Generate, Spreeder's 20,504-book catalogue, Courses content, and sharing.
- **Built from my best guess, because I couldn't see them live:** the Tag, Edit Image, Delete and Edit Profile dialogs; the filled bookmarks list; the charts once there's data; the Light-theme sidebar colours; and the speedometer artwork. Seeing these would have meant creating or deleting things in your Spreeder account, so I left them.

**Your Spreeder account:** I switched it to Light, High Contrast and the other reading modes to capture them, then set it back to Dark and Serial. Opening the passage and briefly playing Scroll moved its saved position forward a few words. Nothing else changed: no bookmarks, no goal change, no text added or deleted.

**Before LSAT U integration:** the LSAT U branch's copy of the feature is now well behind this rebuild. Integrating means porting the standalone version across, not merging the two.

## assistant

Flow, Scroll and Profile now all work. Testing turned up three bugs, now fixed and committed as `e41d2c2`; all 28 tests pass. One thing I couldn't see: the browser pane was hidden, so I never watched Flow's sweep move on screen. I confirmed it from the browser's animation state instead.

**Bugs found and fixed**
- **Blank reader in a background tab.** The reader drew no words and couldn't be played. Its stage size came only from a resize listener that doesn't fire while a tab is hidden. It now measures itself as soon as it opens.
- **Flow's wipe could skip.** When no frame was drawn in between, the mask jumped straight to the end instead of sweeping. It now sweeps every time.
- **Empty Profile charts in a background tab.** The axes showed but the bars and line stayed at zero, because the entrance animation never ran. The charts now draw without it.

**Flow**
- Each chunk appears left-aligned, and the next one comes up after its proper time (about 900 ms for 3 words at 200 WPM).
- The browser confirms a running 894 ms sweep across the chunk.

**Scroll**
- Only the current line shows.
- Each line appears 36px lower than the last.
- After 15 lines it starts again at the top of the page, which is Spreeder's rule for 36px text in this window.

**Profile**
- **Empty dashboard:** both gauges show (0 and 600), and the three chart cards say "Nothing just yet".
- **Dashboard with sample sessions:** all three charts draw, and Current Speed shows 240, which is correct for the sample data.
- **Stats tab:** the metric and period pickers match Spreeder's options. Switching to 24 Hours narrows the chart to today.
- **Options menu:** Theme switches between Light, Dark, High Contrast and System, and Edit Profile renames the heading.
- **Goals:** the dialog text matches Spreeder's. The slider and Set update the Target Speed gauge.
- **Recommend:** the test runs from intro to text to reading. Done stays disabled until you scroll to the end, then it shows your speed, a recommended goal, and Set goals saves it. My test finished in about a second, so the speed came out absurdly high and the goal capped at 2,000, as Spreeder's formula does.
- **Settings:** opens with only Appearance and Advanced, and the right defaults.

**Cleanup.** I removed the sample sessions and put the goal back to 600, with Serial mode and Dark theme. The profile name is now "Joshua" from the Edit Profile test, so the reader's dialog says "Joshua's preferences". Rename it in Edit Profile if you'd like something else.

**What I haven't tested in the browser:** the Add Text screen (Paste and Import), the library ⋮ menu actions (View/Location, Edit Image, Edit Text, Tag, Delete, Hide from Recent), Tags filtering, the bookmark create, edit and list flow, the WPM dialog, keyboard shortcuts, and the finish alert. Want me to run through those next?

## assistant

# Session Handoff — Reading Center rebuilt as a standalone Spreeder copy: reader, library and Profile done; the remaining features still need to be tested against real Spreeder

## Where it started
Joshua pasted the previous handoff, in which the Reading Center build was paused at Step 10 inside LSAT U. He then changed course twice:
- First, put it in a Teacher Workspace "Tools" room.
- Then, build it as an entirely separate web app first and integrate it into LSAT U later. "Assign" will mean granting a student access to the whole tool.

He then required it to match Spreeder exactly in colours, layout and every feature's behaviour, using reverse-engineer-anything as the source of truth. His last two messages, both interrupted, were: "do all of the features and option working and mirror it /reverse-engineer-anything does it flow perfectly and such in the same way as spreeder".

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- **Standalone app.** It lives at `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os-projects\reading-center`: Vite + React + TS + Tailwind, a local git repo with no remote, and data saved in the browser (`src/features/reading-center/lib/store.ts`). Commits: `d2af782` (initial), `441536b` (Spreeder rebuild), `e41d2c2` (fixes for hidden tabs, the Flow mask and chart animation).
- **Tools room reverted** on the LSAT U branch `feat/reading-center`. Revert commit `d5bbd69b` is in the worktree `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os-projects\lsatu-reading-center`. Earlier commits on that branch, unpushed: Step 10 fix `8ff3641c`, steps 11–17 (`3e924766`, `2f88c0d1`, `053212ed`, `c3472072`, `dbbfa1dd`, `8ab5ace4`).
- **The LSAT U copy is out of date.** `src/features/reading-center/` on that branch is now far behind the standalone app. Integrating means porting the standalone code across, not merging.
- **Reconstruction spec.** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\research\2026-10-13-spreeder-reconstruction-spec.md` replaces the visual guidance in the old build spec. It covers the palette for each theme, every screen's measurements, the text model, layout, the timing formula and the dialogs. Section 7 is build status and gaps.
- **Engine ported from Spreeder's shipped code** (`lib/spreeder.ts`):
  - whitespace collapsed, no paragraphs
  - sentence sections of about 2,000–2,400 characters
  - line building by measured width and Words Per Line
  - the `evenChunkDelays`, variability and punctuation (×2) rules
  - remaining time and progress formulas
- **Worker timer** (`lib/workerTimer.ts`). Like Spreeder, it uses a Web Worker because `setTimeout` is clamped to 1 s in hidden tabs.
- **Flow differs from Spreeder on purpose.** Spreeder's mask uses `timeDiff/m`, which is buggy and shows almost nothing. Ours is the intended left-to-right reveal.
- **Brand assets not copied.** No Spreeder logo, cover art or icon font. Ionicons (npm `ionicons@7.4.0`) and Material Icons via Google Fonts stand in.
- **Goal recommendation uses Spreeder's real logic** (`lib/goals.ts`): reading-test WPM, then a piecewise curve with max 2000.
- **Not built** (need a service): Look Up Word, Add to Wordela Word List, AI Book Summary, AI Generate, the 20,504-book catalogue, Courses content, share, Sign Out.
- **Built from inference** (not observed in Spreeder): the filled bookmark list, the Tag, Edit Image, Delete and Edit Profile dialogs, the reading-test text picker, charts with data, the Light-theme sidenav colours, the speedometer artwork.

## Key files for next session
